Read once: existing rules and this review

Interpretation instructions v3.1 and rubric v2 have already been approved. This review selects essential findings for new cases; it does not reopen the shared rules.

Read the actual model input before choosing requirements. Plots are additional expert evidence: do not require unseen waveform details, hidden reference values, or administrative metadata. Mean-relative direction, empirical percentile comparison and clinical normality are different claims. Appropriate uncertainty is allowed.

G03 remains one common requirement per reference-bearing report: limited comparability plus the limit on disease/normality inference. Do not repeat it as a new item in every case. Coverage and errors remain separate. Optional CCI/cadence claims can still be wrong even when not required.

Retain, modify or omit each candidate. Numerical ranking proposes review priorities; it is not a validated importance rule. Add another input-supported finding if needed. Do not add a requirement only because a model later omitted it.

Use this interactive page or the companion Excel workbook, not two conflicting copies. Each expert works independently. Export JSON to preserve and return browser edits; browser storage is not a submission.

These 35 memberships are provisional pending external exposure review. No generated model reports are included.

stroke_Sub01_P

Draft case requirements; participant eligibility remains provisional

Actual model input: read before selecting required findings
Candidate interpretation version: limited-evidence-v3.1-candidate
Draft the four-section report using only this subject's supplied evidence.

Metric definitions (read before interpreting any number below):
- All joint angles and ROM values are SAGITTAL PLANE only. Coronal/transverse data are not included.
- The released EMG curves are ensemble-averaged envelopes. For each participant and recorded muscle, normalization uses a shared maximum across the available strides before averaging; individual strides are not independently rescaled to their own peaks. This pipeline uses the released mean curves without further amplitude normalization. In the checked stroke release, the denominator is participant-, side-, and muscle-specific; it is not shared between sides. The local array audit verified 614 evaluable combinations, not all 700; 86 lacked valid nonzero paired samples. Amplitudes are relative envelope values, not absolute neural activation, force, or strength. Do not rank muscles as stronger or weaker from these amplitudes, or infer stride-to-stride variability from an average curve.
- Above-threshold intervals are the regions where the averaged envelope exceeds 20% of that curve's own peak, minimum retained interval 3% of the cycle (by sample count). An interval written as (X%-Y%) with X > Y wraps across the 100%->0% cycle boundary (envelope remains above threshold across the cycle-defining event). An onset of 0% is the analysis-window boundary, not necessarily a physiological onset. "No interval detected" means no region met the threshold + duration criteria; it does NOT mean the muscle is inactive or non-functional.
- CCI is the Falconer-Winter co-contraction index computed on the full continuous envelopes (2*common area / summed areas), so it can be non-zero even when the DETECTED burst intervals of the pair do not overlap (sub-threshold activity contributes).
- The composite kinematic deviation score is the RMS of the per-joint Gait Variable Scores over the sagittal channels provided ("GPS-like", not the validated 9-variable GPS).
- Step length symmetry index: 200*(paretic - non-paretic)/(paretic + non-paretic), in %. NEGATIVE means the paretic-side step is SHORTER. Two operational step-length definitions exist (successive-footfall: each heel's position at its own initial contact; instantaneous: both heels at the same instant) and give different values for some subjects -- the definition actually used is stated next to the symmetry value.
- "Max |z| deviation" is the LARGEST absolute z-score across all 1001 phase points of the cycle, comparing this curve to the healthy-cohort mean/SD at each phase. This is an extreme-value statistic, NOT a single-observation z-score: in this dataset every one of 109 healthy controls exceeds |z| = 2 on at least one muscle, and per-muscle healthy 95th percentiles for this statistic run 3.65 (ERS) to 4.61 (TA) -- higher still under leave-one-out. Do not label a value abnormal by the conventional 2-SD rule; use the healthy reference range given per muscle where provided.
- Healthy-cohort CCI reference (this dataset): RF-vs-BF median 0.68 (2.5th-97.5th pct 0.48-0.83); TA-vs-GAS median 0.39 (0.24-0.59). Composite score reference: median 5.1, 95th percentile 10.7.
- COHORT BACKGROUND (calibration context, not necessarily the exact reference used for this subject -- the actual reference composition is stated in a dedicated line with the subject data below): the full healthy cohort is 138 subjects for kinematics, effective n = 106-109 per muscle for EMG (29 controls have no usable EMG; completeness varies by age), pooling both body sides and all ages; it is not age- or speed-matched to this subject. A leave-one-out run uses one subject fewer than these counts.
- SPEED SUPPORT LIMIT (applies to most stroke subjects in this dataset): the healthy reference cohort's self-selected walking speeds span 0.80-1.79 m/s. If this subject's gait speed is below 0.80 m/s, NO speed-matched healthy reference exists in this dataset -- 41 of the 50 stroke subjects are below that floor. Deviations from the reference then conflate slow-walking effects with stroke-specific control changes, and the two cannot be separated from these data. Describe deviations from the reference as such; do not attribute them wholly to stroke pathology.
- Additional source fields are outside this input. Age, sex and gait events may be available in the release; time post-stroke, stroke clinical scores and a non-paretic-side designation apply only to the stroke cohort, as applicable. Do not claim every listed field exists for every participant. Distinguish not supplied, not applicable and documented absent recordings.

The released paretic-side cycle runs from initial contact of that limb at 0% to its next initial contact at 100%. The cycle boundary may be called initial contact; the endpoints are successive occurrences, not one instant. No intermediate event or phase boundary is supplied.

Model-visible information boundary: the summary contains mean-curve metrics and algorithm-detected intervals, not individual toe-off, matched normal muscle timing, stride-level variability, raw-signal quality checks, or expert plots. Information not supplied here must not be described as absent from the source dataset. Gait-cycle percentages alone do not identify gait phases.

Subject: Sub01_P (group: stroke, affected side: paretic)
Gait speed: 0.25 m/s (household ambulator (<0.4 m/s)) [The ambulation label denotes a speed band only, not measured walking independence, safety or real-world community mobility.]
Cadence: 64.7 steps/min
Step length symmetry index: -46.3% (step-length definition: successive_footfall)
Composite kinematic deviation score (GPS-like): 15.32
Deviation reference actually used for this subject: 138 healthy subjects (pooled), 1001 phase points, source: main_cohort_excel.

EMG envelope findings:
- GAS: above-threshold interval(s) 86%-76% (wraps cycle boundary), 90% of cycle above threshold, above threshold across cycle boundary, peak normalized amplitude 0.46, max |z| vs healthy reference: 10.7 (above reference, at 68% of cycle)
- RF: above-threshold interval(s) 77%-88%; 92%-66% (wraps cycle boundary), 86% of cycle above threshold, above threshold across cycle boundary, peak normalized amplitude 0.50, max |z| vs healthy reference: 4.3 (above reference, at 81% of cycle)
- VL: above-threshold interval(s) 92%-63% (wraps cycle boundary), 71% of cycle above threshold, above threshold across cycle boundary, peak normalized amplitude 0.45, max |z| vs healthy reference: 4.2 (above reference, at 34% of cycle)
- BF: above-threshold interval(s) 42%-50%; 50%-58%; 60%-76%; 82%-39% (wraps cycle boundary), 89% of cycle above threshold, above threshold across cycle boundary, peak normalized amplitude 0.55, max |z| vs healthy reference: 2.3 (above reference, at 21% of cycle)
- ST: above-threshold interval(s) 42%-46%; 51%-55%; 58%-76%; 88%-37% (wraps cycle boundary), 74% of cycle above threshold, above threshold across cycle boundary, peak normalized amplitude 0.56, max |z| vs healthy reference: 4.1 (above reference, at 24% of cycle)
- TA: above-threshold interval(s) 12%-63%; 75%-6% (wraps cycle boundary), 82% of cycle above threshold, above threshold across cycle boundary, peak normalized amplitude 0.51, max |z| vs healthy reference: 4.1 (above reference, at 37% of cycle)
- ERS: above-threshold interval(s) 0%-100%, 100% of cycle above threshold, above threshold across cycle boundary, peak normalized amplitude 0.63, max |z| vs healthy reference: 7.7 (above reference, at 78% of cycle)

Co-contraction indices:
- TA_vs_GAS: CCI = 0.67
- RF_vs_BF: CCI = 0.72

Joint kinematic findings (sagittal plane):
- AnkleAngles: range of motion 11.6 deg, Gait Variable Score 16.27
- KneeAngles: range of motion 54.3 deg, Gait Variable Score 22.25
- HipAngles: range of motion 46.8 deg, Gait Variable Score 11.32
- PelvisAngles: range of motion 11.4 deg, Gait Variable Score 7.17
System instructions
Draft a research gait-report interpretation for a physical therapist
or physiatrist using only the supplied quantitative summary. This is a limited-evidence
interpretation task, not diagnosis or treatment selection.

Rules:
- Describe the participant's actual group and side, available findings, and missing
  channels accurately. Healthy-group membership does not prove normal physiology;
  deviations from an empirical reference do not by themselves establish disease.
- Treat detected EMG intervals and coverage as properties of an averaged envelope
  under the stated threshold and duration rule. Do not convert them into physiological
  onset, offset, duration, continuous contraction, or inactivity outside the intervals.
  You may describe reported coverage, retained intervals and boundary continuity at
  the mean-envelope level. Do not infer the same pattern in every individual stride.
  Say no below-threshold segment only when the supplied evidence establishes that;
  a rounded coverage value or no retained interval alone does not establish it.
- Individual toe-off and matched muscle-specific timing references are not supplied
  in this task. Use percentages of the analysis cycle. When the input explicitly
  identifies an initial-contact boundary, you may name that boundary or describe
  numerical proximity to it. Do not assign stance/swing, loading response, pre-swing,
  mid-stance or another event to a percentage, or substitute a conventional 60% split.
  Anatomical muscle actions may be named, but do not map an interval to a gait-phase
  function such as clearance, push-off or weight acceptance. Do not characterize
  timing as normal, early, delayed or prolonged without the required evidence.
- Respect the supplied normalization method. Relative envelope amplitude is not
  strength or force, and average curves do not establish stride-to-stride variability.
- Report CCI as a whole-cycle overlap index of normalized EMG envelopes. You may
  compare it with the supplied empirical interval or a supplied median, including
  proximity to an endpoint. Do not invent an unavailable median. Being within that
  interval does not establish physiologically normal co-activation or normal muscle
  function; being outside it does not establish pathological co-activation, stiffness,
  impaired control or a diagnosis. CCI is not simultaneous activation time, muscular
  force or a phase-specific result. It depends on normalization and envelope methods;
  do not transfer values or thresholds from other studies or algorithms.
- Describe GVS and GPS-like as summary differences from the stated reference.
  Neither localizes waveform direction or phase; ROM alone does not establish a
  clinical restriction without an appropriate comparison. Do not treat pooled
  calibration percentiles as matched leave-one-out normal limits.
- Where using a reference comparison, convey both limited comparability and the
  inability to establish a disease effect or clinical diagnosis from that comparison.
  One clear explanation per report is enough; age and speed need not be recited as
  separate checklist words. A speed inside an overall range does not prove that
  an appropriate matched reference exists. Apply these limits to controls as well.
- The location of maximum |z| is the largest standardized difference from the
  reference, not necessarily the time of peak envelope amplitude or a gait event.
  Above/below reference in an EMG row means relative to the phase-specific reference
  mean at that location, not relative to a healthy percentile limit.
- Preserve numerical direction and the displayed precision. Do not invent values,
  clinical severity grades, statistical significance, or unavailable reference values.
  Descriptive comparisons with supplied values remain allowed. Near-boundary rounded
  values do not justify classifying hidden unrounded values.
- Clinical follow-up questions may ask whether a mechanism or measurement issue
  exists; do not presuppose it has already been observed. Do not recommend treatment.

If EMG is unavailable, section 2 may simply state that envelope timing, amplitude
and CCI cannot be assessed. Reference-only plots do not provide patient EMG.

Use exactly these four sections:
1. Objective Findings
2. EMG Envelope and Threshold Findings
3. Kinematic and Reference Comparisons
4. Questions for Further Clinical Assessment
Supplementary curves and reference context (not model inputs)
# Reference context: stroke/Sub01_P

Mean/SD reference: 138 controls; method pooled; exclusions: [].

Effective reference counts: GAS=109, RF=108, VL=109, BF=106, ST=108, TA=107, ERS=106, AnkleAngles=138, KneeAngles=138, HipAngles=138, PelvisAngles=138.

The exact system and user input files define what the model receives. The EMG and kinematic plots are supplementary expert evidence, not model inputs.
The plotted mean plus/minus one SD is descriptive, not a clinical normal band. Negative lower EMG bands can result from mean minus SD; they do not imply negative recorded EMG.
The max-|z| p95 span in the input is pooled calibration context. Complete muscle-specific LOO thresholds are not supplied; an appropriately indeterminate classification is allowed.
Controls are excluded from their own mean/SD reference, but cohort-level calibration has prior reference-data reuse. It is not an independent matched clinical limit.
GPS-like is a four-channel sagittal composite, not the validated nine-variable GPS. The pooled median/p95 background is not a matched LOO normality boundary.
CCI uses continuous normalized envelopes over the whole analysis cycle. An empirical interval comparison does not establish normal muscle function.
Threshold intervals and rounded coverage describe the averaged envelope. Rounded 100% coverage and one interval do not prove absence of short below-threshold gaps.
Use the group-specific cycle and normalization provenance in the actual input. No individual intermediate gait events or matched normal muscle timing are supplied.
Administrative age and published bilateral speed are not added to this input. Expert-visible plots do not justify requiring unseen direction, phase or morphology.

For a new-case requirement, cite exact input evidence. Select clinically useful descriptive content without inferring a diagnosis or severity from rank alone.

- ERS: above-threshold interval(s) 44%-34% (wraps cycle boundary), 90% of cycle above threshold, above threshold across cycle boundary, peak normalized amplitude 0.71, max |z| vs healthy reference: 5.5 (above reference, at 91% of cycle)

stroke_Sub07_P

Draft case requirements; participant eligibility remains provisional

Actual model input: read before selecting required findings
Candidate interpretation version: limited-evidence-v3.1-candidate
Draft the four-section report using only this subject's supplied evidence.

- CCI is the Falconer-Winter co-contraction index computed on the full continuous envelopes (2*common area / summed areas), so it can be non-zero even when the DETECTED burst intervals of the pair do not overlap (sub-threshold activity contributes).
- The composite kinematic deviation score is the RMS of the per-joint Gait Variable Scores over the sagittal channels provided ("GPS-like", not the validated 9-variable GPS).
- Step length symmetry index: 200*(paretic - non-paretic)/(paretic + non-paretic), in %. NEGATIVE means the paretic-side step is SHORTER. Two operational step-length definitions exist (successive-footfall: each heel's position at its own initial contact; instantaneous: both heels at the same instant) and give different values for some subjects -- the definition actually used is stated next to the symmetry value.
- "Max |z| deviation" is the LARGEST absolute z-score across all 1001 phase points of the cycle, comparing this curve to the healthy-cohort mean/SD at each phase. This is an extreme-value statistic, NOT a single-observation z-score: in this dataset every one of 109 healthy controls exceeds |z| = 2 on at least one muscle, and per-muscle healthy 95th percentiles for this statistic run 3.65 (ERS) to 4.61 (TA) -- higher still under leave-one-out. Do not label a value abnormal by the conventional 2-SD rule; use the healthy reference range given per muscle where provided.
- Healthy-cohort CCI reference (this dataset): RF-vs-BF median 0.68 (2.5th-97.5th pct 0.48-0.83); TA-vs-GAS median 0.39 (0.24-0.59). Composite score reference: median 5.1, 95th percentile 10.7.
- COHORT BACKGROUND (calibration context, not necessarily the exact reference used for this subject -- the actual reference composition is stated in a dedicated line with the subject data below): the full healthy cohort is 138 subjects for kinematics, effective n = 106-109 per muscle for EMG (29 controls have no usable EMG; completeness varies by age), pooling both body sides and all ages; it is not age- or speed-matched to this subject. A leave-one-out run uses one subject fewer than these counts.
- SPEED SUPPORT LIMIT (applies to most stroke subjects in this dataset): the healthy reference cohort's self-selected walking speeds span 0.80-1.79 m/s. If this subject's gait speed is below 0.80 m/s, NO speed-matched healthy reference exists in this dataset -- 41 of the 50 stroke subjects are below that floor. Deviations from the reference then conflate slow-walking effects with stroke-specific control changes, and the two cannot be separated from these data. Describe deviations from the reference as such; do not attribute them wholly to stroke pathology.
- Additional source fields are outside this input. Age, sex and gait events may be available in the release; time post-stroke, stroke clinical scores and a non-paretic-side designation apply only to the stroke cohort, as applicable. Do not claim every listed field exists for every participant. Distinguish not supplied, not applicable and documented absent recordings.

The released paretic-side cycle runs from initial contact of that limb at 0% to its next initial contact at 100%. The cycle boundary may be called initial contact; the endpoints are successive occurrences, not one instant. No intermediate event or phase boundary is supplied.

Model-visible information boundary: the summary contains mean-curve metrics and algorithm-detected intervals, not individual toe-off, matched normal muscle timing, stride-level variability, raw-signal quality checks, or expert plots. Information not supplied here must not be described as absent from the source dataset. Gait-cycle percentages alone do not identify gait phases.

System instructions
Draft a research gait-report interpretation for a physical therapist
or physiatrist using only the supplied quantitative summary. This is a limited-evidence
interpretation task, not diagnosis or treatment selection.

Rules:
- Describe the participant's actual group and side, available findings, and missing
  channels accurately. Healthy-group membership does not prove normal physiology;
  deviations from an empirical reference do not by themselves establish disease.
- Treat detected EMG intervals and coverage as properties of an averaged envelope
  under the stated threshold and duration rule. Do not convert them into physiological
  onset, offset, duration, continuous contraction, or inactivity outside the intervals.
  You may describe reported coverage, retained intervals and boundary continuity at
  the mean-envelope level. Do not infer the same pattern in every individual stride.
  Say no below-threshold segment only when the supplied evidence establishes that;
  a rounded coverage value or no retained interval alone does not establish it.
- Individual toe-off and matched muscle-specific timing references are not supplied
  in this task. Use percentages of the analysis cycle. When the input explicitly
  identifies an initial-contact boundary, you may name that boundary or describe
  numerical proximity to it. Do not assign stance/swing, loading response, pre-swing,
  mid-stance or another event to a percentage, or substitute a conventional 60% split.
  Anatomical muscle actions may be named, but do not map an interval to a gait-phase
  function such as clearance, push-off or weight acceptance. Do not characterize
  timing as normal, early, delayed or prolonged without the required evidence.
- Respect the supplied normalization method. Relative envelope amplitude is not
  strength or force, and average curves do not establish stride-to-stride variability.
- Report CCI as a whole-cycle overlap index of normalized EMG envelopes. You may
  compare it with the supplied empirical interval or a supplied median, including
  proximity to an endpoint. Do not invent an unavailable median. Being within that
  interval does not establish physiologically normal co-activation or normal muscle
  function; being outside it does not establish pathological co-activation, stiffness,
  impaired control or a diagnosis. CCI is not simultaneous activation time, muscular
  force or a phase-specific result. It depends on normalization and envelope methods;
  do not transfer values or thresholds from other studies or algorithms.
- Describe GVS and GPS-like as summary differences from the stated reference.
  Neither localizes waveform direction or phase; ROM alone does not establish a
  clinical restriction without an appropriate comparison. Do not treat pooled
  calibration percentiles as matched leave-one-out normal limits.
- Where using a reference comparison, convey both limited comparability and the
  inability to establish a disease effect or clinical diagnosis from that comparison.
  One clear explanation per report is enough; age and speed need not be recited as
  separate checklist words. A speed inside an overall range does not prove that
  an appropriate matched reference exists. Apply these limits to controls as well.
- The location of maximum |z| is the largest standardized difference from the
  reference, not necessarily the time of peak envelope amplitude or a gait event.
  Above/below reference in an EMG row means relative to the phase-specific reference
  mean at that location, not relative to a healthy percentile limit.
- Preserve numerical direction and the displayed precision. Do not invent values,
  clinical severity grades, statistical significance, or unavailable reference values.
  Descriptive comparisons with supplied values remain allowed. Near-boundary rounded
  values do not justify classifying hidden unrounded values.
- Clinical follow-up questions may ask whether a mechanism or measurement issue
  exists; do not presuppose it has already been observed. Do not recommend treatment.

If EMG is unavailable, section 2 may simply state that envelope timing, amplitude
and CCI cannot be assessed. Reference-only plots do not provide patient EMG.

Use exactly these four sections:
1. Objective Findings
2. EMG Envelope and Threshold Findings
3. Kinematic and Reference Comparisons
4. Questions for Further Clinical Assessment

# Reference context: stroke/Sub07_P

The exact system and user input files define what the model receives. The EMG and kinematic plots are supplementary expert evidence, not model inputs.
The plotted mean plus/minus one SD is descriptive, not a clinical normal band. Negative lower EMG bands can result from mean minus SD; they do not imply negative recorded EMG.
The max-|z| p95 span in the input is pooled calibration context. Complete muscle-specific LOO thresholds are not supplied; an appropriately indeterminate classification is allowed.
Controls are excluded from their own mean/SD reference, but cohort-level calibration has prior reference-data reuse. It is not an independent matched clinical limit.
GPS-like is a four-channel sagittal composite, not the validated nine-variable GPS. The pooled median/p95 background is not a matched LOO normality boundary.
CCI uses continuous normalized envelopes over the whole analysis cycle. An empirical interval comparison does not establish normal muscle function.
Threshold intervals and rounded coverage describe the averaged envelope. Rounded 100% coverage and one interval do not prove absence of short below-threshold gaps.
Use the group-specific cycle and normalization provenance in the actual input. No individual intermediate gait events or matched normal muscle timing are supplied.
Administrative age and published bilateral speed are not added to this input. Expert-visible plots do not justify requiring unseen direction, phase or morphology.

Metric definitions (read before interpreting any number below):
- All joint angles and ROM values are SAGITTAL PLANE only. Coronal/transverse data are not included.
- The released EMG curves are ensemble-averaged envelopes. For each participant and recorded muscle, normalization uses a shared maximum across the available strides before averaging; individual strides are not independently rescaled to their own peaks. This pipeline uses the released mean curves without further amplitude normalization. In the checked stroke release, the denominator is participant-, side-, and muscle-specific; it is not shared between sides. The local array audit verified 614 evaluable combinations, not all 700; 86 lacked valid nonzero paired samples. Amplitudes are relative envelope values, not absolute neural activation, force, or strength. Do not rank muscles as stronger or weaker from these amplitudes, or infer stride-to-stride variability from an average curve.
- Above-threshold intervals are the regions where the averaged envelope exceeds 20% of that curve's own peak, minimum retained interval 3% of the cycle (by sample count). An interval written as (X%-Y%) with X > Y wraps across the 100%->0% cycle boundary (envelope remains above threshold across the cycle-defining event). An onset of 0% is the analysis-window boundary, not necessarily a physiological onset. "No interval detected" means no region met the threshold + duration criteria; it does NOT mean the muscle is inactive or non-functional.
- CCI is the Falconer-Winter co-contraction index computed on the full continuous envelopes (2*common area / summed areas), so it can be non-zero even when the DETECTED burst intervals of the pair do not overlap (sub-threshold activity contributes).
- The composite kinematic deviation score is the RMS of the per-joint Gait Variable Scores over the sagittal channels provided ("GPS-like", not the validated 9-variable GPS).
- Step length symmetry index: 200*(paretic - non-paretic)/(paretic + non-paretic), in %. NEGATIVE means the paretic-side step is SHORTER. Two operational step-length definitions exist (successive-footfall: each heel's position at its own initial contact; instantaneous: both heels at the same instant) and give different values for some subjects -- the definition actually used is stated next to the symmetry value.
- "Max |z| deviation" is the LARGEST absolute z-score across all 1001 phase points of the cycle, comparing this curve to the healthy-cohort mean/SD at each phase. This is an extreme-value statistic, NOT a single-observation z-score: in this dataset every one of 109 healthy controls exceeds |z| = 2 on at least one muscle, and per-muscle healthy 95th percentiles for this statistic run 3.65 (ERS) to 4.61 (TA) -- higher still under leave-one-out. System instructions
Draft a research gait-report interpretation for a physical therapist
or physiatrist using only the supplied quantitative summary. This is a limited-evidence
interpretation task, not diagnosis or treatment selection.

Rules:
- Describe the participant's actual group and side, available findings, and missing
  channels accurately. Healthy-group membership does not prove normal physiology;
  deviations from an empirical reference do not by themselves establish disease.
- Treat detected EMG intervals and coverage as properties of an averaged envelope
  under the stated threshold and duration rule. Do not convert them into physiological
  onset, offset, duration, continuous contraction, or inactivity outside the intervals.
  You may describe reported coverage, retained intervals and boundary continuity at
  the mean-envelope level. Do not infer the same pattern in every individual stride.
  Say no below-threshold segment only when the supplied evidence establishes that;
  a rounded coverage value or no retained interval alone does not establish it.
- Individual toe-off and matched muscle-specific timing references are not supplied
  in this task. Use percentages of the analysis cycle. When the input explicitly
  identifies an initial-contact boundary, you may name that boundary or describe
  numerical proximity to it. Do not assign stance/swing, loading response, pre-swing,
  mid-stance or another event to a percentage, or substitute a conventional 60% split.
  Anatomical muscle actions may be named, but do not map an interval to a gait-phase
  function such as clearance, push-off or weight acceptance. Do not characterize
  timing as normal, early, delayed or prolonged without the required evidence.
- Respect the supplied normalization method. Relative envelope amplitude is not
  strength or force, and average curves do not establish stride-to-stride variability.
- Report CCI as a whole-cycle overlap index of normalized EMG envelopes. You may
  compare it with the supplied empirical interval or a supplied median, including
  proximity to an endpoint. Do not invent an unavailable median. Being within that
  interval does not establish physiologically normal co-activation or normal muscle
  function; being outside it does not establish pathological co-activation, stiffness,
  impaired control or a diagnosis. CCI is not simultaneous activation time, muscular
  force or a phase-specific result. It depends on normalization and envelope methods;
  do not transfer values or thresholds from other studies or algorithms.
- Describe GVS and GPS-like as summary differences from the stated reference.
  Neither localizes waveform direction or phase; ROM alone does not establish a
  clinical restriction without an appropriate comparison. Do not treat pooled
  calibration percentiles as matched leave-one-out normal limits.
- Where using a reference comparison, convey both limited comparability and the
  inability to establish a disease effect or clinical diagnosis from that comparison.
  One clear explanation per report is enough; age and speed need not be recited as
  separate checklist words. A speed inside an overall range does not prove that
  an appropriate matched reference exists. Apply these limits to controls as well.
- The location of maximum |z| is the largest standardized difference from the
  reference, not necessarily the time of peak envelope amplitude or a gait event.
  Above/below reference in an EMG row means relative to the phase-specific reference
  mean at that location, not relative to a healthy percentile limit.
- Preserve numerical direction and the displayed precision. Do not invent values,
  clinical severity grades, statistical significance, or unavailable reference values.
  Descriptive comparisons with supplied values remain allowed. Near-boundary rounded
  values do not justify classifying hidden unrounded values.
- Clinical follow-up questions may ask whether a mechanism or measurement issue
  exists; do not presuppose it has already been observed. Do not recommend treatment.

If EMG is unavailable, section 2 may simply state that envelope timing, amplitude
and CCI cannot be assessed. Reference-only plots do not provide patient EMG.

Use exactly these four sections:
1. Objective Findings
2. EMG Envelope and Threshold Findings
3. Kinematic and Reference Comparisons
4. Questions for Further Clinical Assessment
Supplementary curves and reference context (not model inputs)
# Reference context: stroke/Sub13_P

Mean/SD reference: 138 controls; method pooled; exclusions: [].

Effective reference counts: GAS=109, RF=108, VL=109, BF=106, ST=108, TA=107, ERS=106, AnkleAngles=138, KneeAngles=138, HipAngles=138, PelvisAngles=138.

The exact system and user input files define what the model receives. The EMG and kinematic plots are supplementary expert evidence, not model inputs.
The plotted mean plus/minus one SD is descriptive, not a clinical normal band. Negative lower EMG bands can result from mean minus SD; they do not imply negative recorded EMG.
The max-|z| p95 span in the input is pooled calibration context. Complete muscle-specific LOO thresholds are not supplied; an appropriately indeterminate classification is allowed.
Controls are excluded from their own mean/SD reference, but cohort-level calibration has prior reference-data reuse. It is not an independent matched clinical limit.
GPS-like is a four-channel sagittal composite, not the validated nine-variable GPS. The pooled median/p95 background is not a matched LOO normality boundary.
CCI uses continuous normalized envelopes over the whole analysis cycle. An empirical interval comparison does not establish normal muscle function.
Threshold intervals and rounded coverage describe the averaged envelope. Rounded 100% coverage and one interval do not prove absence of short below-threshold gaps.
Use the group-specific cycle and normalization provenance in the actual input. No individual intermediate gait events or matched normal muscle timing are supplied.
Administrative age and published bilateral speed are not added to this input. Expert-visible plots do not justify requiring unseen direction, phase or morphology.

For a new-case requirement, cite exact input evidence. Select clinically useful descriptive content without inferring a diagnosis or severity from rank alone.

stroke_Sub15_P

Draft case requirements; participant eligibility remains provisional

Actual model input: read before selecting required findings
Candidate interpretation version: limited-evidence-v3.1-candidate
Draft the four-section report using only this subject's supplied evidence.

Metric definitions (read before interpreting any number below):
- All joint angles and ROM values are SAGITTAL PLANE only. Coronal/transverse data are not included.
- The released EMG curves are ensemble-averaged envelopes. For each participant and recorded muscle, normalization uses a shared maximum across the available strides before averaging; individual strides are not independently rescaled to their own peaks. This pipeline uses the released mean curves without further amplitude normalization. In the checked stroke release, the denominator is participant-, side-, and muscle-specific; it is not shared between sides. The local array audit verified 614 evaluable combinations, not all 700; 86 lacked valid nonzero paired samples. Amplitudes are relative envelope values, not absolute neural activation, force, or strength. Do not rank muscles as stronger or weaker from these amplitudes, or infer stride-to-stride variability from an average curve.
- Above-threshold intervals are the regions where the averaged envelope exceeds 20% of that curve's own peak, minimum retained interval 3% of the cycle (by sample count). An interval written as (X%-Y%) with X > Y wraps across the 100%->0% cycle boundary (envelope remains above threshold across the cycle-defining event). An onset of 0% is the analysis-window boundary, not necessarily a physiological onset. "No interval detected" means no region met the threshold + duration criteria; it does NOT mean the muscle is inactive or non-functional.
- CCI is the Falconer-Winter co-contraction index computed on the full continuous envelopes (2*common area / summed areas), so it can be non-zero even when the DETECTED burst intervals of the pair do not overlap (sub-threshold activity contributes).
- The composite kinematic deviation score is the RMS of the per-joint Gait Variable Scores over the sagittal channels provided ("GPS-like", not the validated 9-variable GPS).
- Step length symmetry index: 200*(paretic - non-paretic)/(paretic + non-paretic), in %. NEGATIVE means the paretic-side step is SHORTER. Two operational step-length definitions exist (successive-footfall: each heel's position at its own initial contact; instantaneous: both heels at the same instant) and give different values for some subjects -- the definition actually used is stated next to the symmetry value.
- "Max |z| deviation" is the LARGEST absolute z-score across all 1001 phase points of the cycle, comparing this curve to the healthy-cohort mean/SD at each phase. This is an extreme-value statistic, NOT a single-observation z-score: in this dataset every one of 109 healthy controls exceeds |z| = 2 on at least one muscle, and per-muscle healthy 95th percentiles for this statistic run 3.65 (ERS) to 4.61 (TA) -- higher still under leave-one-out. Do not label a value abnormal by the conventional 2-SD rule; use the healthy reference range given per muscle where provided.
- Healthy-cohort CCI reference (this dataset): RF-vs-BF median 0.68 (2.5th-97.5th pct 0.48-0.83); TA-vs-GAS median 0.39 (0.24-0.59). Composite score reference: median 5.1, 95th percentile 10.7.
- COHORT BACKGROUND (calibration context, not necessarily the exact reference used for this subject -- the actual reference composition is stated in a dedicated line with the subject data below): the full healthy cohort is 138 subjects for kinematics, effective n = 106-109 per muscle for EMG (29 controls have no usable EMG; completeness varies by age), pooling both body sides and all ages; it is not age- or speed-matched to this subject. A leave-one-out run uses one subject fewer than these counts.
- SPEED SUPPORT LIMIT (applies to most stroke subjects in this dataset): the healthy reference cohort's self-selected walking speeds span 0.80-1.79 m/s. If this subject's gait speed is below 0.80 m/s, NO speed-matched healthy reference exists in this dataset -- 41 of the 50 stroke subjects are below that floor. Deviations from the reference then conflate slow-walking effects with stroke-specific control changes, and the two cannot be separated from these data. Describe deviations from the reference as such; do not attribute them wholly to stroke pathology.
- Additional source fields are outside this input. Age, sex and gait events may be available in the release; time post-stroke, stroke clinical scores and a non-paretic-side designation apply only to the stroke cohort, as applicable. Do not claim every listed field exists for every participant. Distinguish not supplied, not applicable and documented absent recordings.

The released paretic-side cycle runs from initial contact of that limb at 0% to its next initial contact at 100%. The cycle boundary may be called initial contact; the endpoints are successive occurrences, not one instant. No intermediate event or phase boundary is supplied.

Model-visible information boundary: the summary contains mean-curve metrics and algorithm-detected intervals, not individual toe-off, matched normal muscle timing, stride-level variability, raw-signal quality checks, or expert plots. Information not supplied here must not be described as absent from the source dataset. Gait-cycle percentages alone do not identify gait phases.

  mean at that location, not relative to a healthy percentile limit.
- Preserve numerical direction and the displayed precision. Do not invent values,
  clinical severity grades, statistical significance, or unavailable reference values.
  Descriptive comparisons with supplied values remain allowed. Near-boundary rounded
  values do not justify classifying hidden unrounded values.
- Clinical follow-up questions may ask whether a mechanism or measurement issue
  exists; do not presuppose it has already been observed. Do not recommend treatment.

If EMG is unavailable, section 2 may simply state that envelope timing, amplitude
and CCI cannot be assessed. Reference-only plots do not provide patient EMG.

Use exactly these four sections:
1. Objective Findings
2. EMG Envelope and Threshold Findings
3. Kinematic and Reference Comparisons
4. Questions for Further Clinical Assessment

# Reference context: stroke/Sub16_P

Mean/SD reference: 138 controls; method pooled; exclusions: [].

Effective reference counts: GAS=109, RF=108, VL=109, BF=106, ST=108, TA=107, ERS=106, AnkleAngles=138, KneeAngles=138, HipAngles=138, PelvisAngles=138.

The exact system and user input files define what the model receives. The EMG and kinematic plots are supplementary expert evidence, not model inputs.
The plotted mean plus/minus one SD is descriptive, not a clinical normal band. Negative lower EMG bands can result from mean minus SD; they do not imply negative recorded EMG.
The max-|z| p95 span in the input is pooled calibration context. Complete muscle-specific LOO thresholds are not supplied; an appropriately indeterminate classification is allowed.
Controls are excluded from their own mean/SD reference, but cohort-level calibration has prior reference-data reuse. It is not an independent matched clinical limit.
GPS-like is a four-channel sagittal composite, not the validated nine-variable GPS. The pooled median/p95 background is not a matched LOO normality boundary.
CCI uses continuous normalized envelopes over the whole analysis cycle. An empirical interval comparison does not establish normal muscle function.
Threshold intervals and rounded coverage describe the averaged envelope. Rounded 100% coverage and one interval do not prove absence of short below-threshold gaps.
Use the group-specific cycle and normalization provenance in the actual input. No individual intermediate gait events or matched normal muscle timing are supplied.
Administrative age and published bilateral speed are not added to this input. Expert-visible plots do not justify requiring unseen direction, phase or morphology.

stroke_Sub18_P

Draft case requirements; participant eligibility remains provisional

Actual model input: read before selecting required findings
Candidate interpretation version: limited-evidence-v3.1-candidate
Draft the four-section report using only this subject's supplied evidence.

Metric definitions (read before interpreting any number below):
- All joint angles and ROM values are SAGITTAL PLANE only. Coronal/transverse data are not included.
- The released EMG curves are ensemble-averaged envelopes. For each participant and recorded muscle, normalization uses a shared maximum across the available strides before averaging; individual strides are not independently rescaled to their own peaks. This pipeline uses the released mean curves without further amplitude normalization. In the checked stroke release, the denominator is participant-, side-, and muscle-specific; it is not shared between sides. The local array audit verified 614 evaluable combinations, not all 700; 86 lacked valid nonzero paired samples. Amplitudes are relative envelope values, not absolute neural activation, force, or strength. Do not rank muscles as stronger or weaker from these amplitudes, or infer stride-to-stride variability from an average curve.
- Above-threshold intervals are the regions where the averaged envelope exceeds 20% of that curve's own peak, minimum retained interval 3% of the cycle (by sample count). An interval written as (X%-Y%) with X > Y wraps across the 100%->0% cycle boundary (envelope remains above threshold across the cycle-defining event). An onset of 0% is the analysis-window boundary, not necessarily a physiological onset. "No interval detected" means no region met the threshold + duration criteria; it does NOT mean the muscle is inactive or non-functional.
- CCI is the Falconer-Winter co-contraction index computed on the full continuous envelopes (2*common area / summed areas), so it can be non-zero even when the DETECTED burst intervals of the pair do not overlap (sub-threshold activity contributes).
- The composite kinematic deviation score is the RMS of the per-joint Gait Variable Scores over the sagittal channels provided ("GPS-like", not the validated 9-variable GPS).
- Step length symmetry index: 200*(paretic - non-paretic)/(paretic + non-paretic), in %. NEGATIVE means the paretic-side step is SHORTER. Two operational step-length definitions exist (successive-footfall: each heel's position at its own initial contact; instantaneous: both heels at the same instant) and give different values for some subjects -- the definition actually used is stated next to the symmetry value.
- "Max |z| deviation" is the LARGEST absolute z-score across all 1001 phase points of the cycle, comparing this curve to the healthy-cohort mean/SD at each phase. This is an extreme-value statistic, NOT a single-observation z-score: in this dataset every one of 109 healthy controls exceeds |z| = 2 on at least one muscle, and per-muscle healthy 95th percentiles for this statistic run 3.65 (ERS) to 4.61 (TA) -- higher still under leave-one-out. Do not label a value abnormal by the conventional 2-SD rule; use the healthy reference range given per muscle where provided.
- Healthy-cohort CCI reference (this dataset): RF-vs-BF median 0.68 (2.5th-97.5th pct 0.48-0.83); TA-vs-GAS median 0.39 (0.24-0.59). Composite score reference: median 5.1, 95th percentile 10.7.
- COHORT BACKGROUND (calibration context, not necessarily the exact reference used for this subject -- the actual reference composition is stated in a dedicated line with the subject data below): the full healthy cohort is 138 subjects for kinematics, effective n = 106-109 per muscle for EMG (29 controls have no usable EMG; completeness varies by age), pooling both body sides and all ages; it is not age- or speed-matched to this subject. A leave-one-out run uses one subject fewer than these counts.
- SPEED SUPPORT LIMIT (applies to most stroke subjects in this dataset): the healthy reference cohort's self-selected walking speeds span 0.80-1.79 m/s. If this subject's gait speed is below 0.80 m/s, NO speed-matched healthy reference exists in this dataset -- 41 of the 50 stroke subjects are below that floor. Deviations from the reference then conflate slow-walking effects with stroke-specific control changes, and the two cannot be separated from these data. Describe deviations from the reference as such; do not attribute them wholly to stroke pathology.
- Additional source fields are outside this input. Age, sex and gait events may be available in the release; time post-stroke, stroke clinical scores and a non-paretic-side designation apply only to the stroke cohort, as applicable. Do not claim every listed field exists for every participant. Distinguish not supplied, not applicable and documented absent recordings.

The released paretic-side cycle runs from initial contact of that limb at 0% to its next initial contact at 100%. The cycle boundary may be called initial contact; the endpoints are successive occurrences, not one instant. No intermediate event or phase boundary is supplied.

Model-visible information boundary: the summary contains mean-curve metrics and algorithm-detected intervals, not individual toe-off, matched normal muscle timing, stride-level variability, raw-signal quality checks, or expert plots. Information not supplied here must not be described as absent from the source dataset. Gait-cycle percentages alone do not identify gait phases.

Cadence: 62.2 steps/min
Step length symmetry index: -3.9% (step-length definition: successive_footfall)

EMG envelope findings:

System instructions
Draft a research gait-report interpretation for a physical therapist
or physiatrist using only the supplied quantitative summary. This is a limited-evidence
interpretation task, not diagnosis or treatment selection.

Rules:
- Describe the participant's actual group and side, available findings, and missing
  channels accurately. Healthy-group membership does not prove normal physiology;
  deviations from an empirical reference do not by themselves establish disease.
- Treat detected EMG intervals and coverage as properties of an averaged envelope
  under the stated threshold and duration rule. Do not convert them into physiological
  onset, offset, duration, continuous contraction, or inactivity outside the intervals.
  You may describe reported coverage, retained intervals and boundary continuity at
  the mean-envelope level. Do not infer the same pattern in every individual stride.
  Say no below-threshold segment only when the supplied evidence establishes that;
  a rounded coverage value or no retained interval alone does not establish it.
- Individual toe-off and matched muscle-specific timing references are not supplied
  in this task. Use percentages of the analysis cycle. When the input explicitly
  identifies an initial-contact boundary, you may name that boundary or describe
  numerical proximity to it. Do not assign stance/swing, loading response, pre-swing,
  mid-stance or another event to a percentage, or substitute a conventional 60% split.
  Anatomical muscle actions may be named, but do not map an interval to a gait-phase
  function such as clearance, push-off or weight acceptance. Do not characterize
  timing as normal, early, delayed or prolonged without the required evidence.
- Respect the supplied normalization method. Relative envelope amplitude is not
  strength or force, and average curves do not establish stride-to-stride variability.
- Report CCI as a whole-cycle overlap index of normalized EMG envelopes. You may
  compare it with the supplied empirical interval or a supplied median, including
  proximity to an endpoint. Do not invent an unavailable median. Being within that
  interval does not establish physiologically normal co-activation or normal muscle
  function; being outside it does not establish pathological co-activation, stiffness,
  impaired control or a diagnosis. CCI is not simultaneous activation time, muscular
  force or a phase-specific result. It depends on normalization and envelope methods;
  do not transfer values or thresholds from other studies or algorithms.
- Describe GVS and GPS-like as summary differences from the stated reference.
  Neither localizes waveform direction or phase; ROM alone does not establish a
  clinical restriction without an appropriate comparison. Do not treat pooled
  calibration percentiles as matched leave-one-out normal limits.
- Where using a reference comparison, convey both limited comparability and the
  inability to establish a disease effect or clinical diagnosis from that comparison.
  One clear explanation per report is enough; age and speed need not be recited as
  separate checklist words. A speed inside an overall range does not prove that
  an appropriate matched reference exists. Apply these limits to controls as well.
- The location of maximum |z| is the largest standardized difference from the
  reference, not necessarily the time of peak envelope amplitude or a gait event.
  Above/below reference in an EMG row means relative to the phase-specific reference
  mean at that location, not relative to a healthy percentile limit.
- Preserve numerical direction and the displayed precision. Do not invent values,
  clinical severity grades, statistical significance, or unavailable reference values.
  Descriptive comparisons with supplied values remain allowed. Near-boundary rounded
  values do not justify classifying hidden unrounded values.
- Clinical follow-up questions may ask whether a mechanism or measurement issue
  exists; do not presuppose it has already been observed. Do not recommend treatment.

If EMG is unavailable, section 2 may simply state that envelope timing, amplitude
and CCI cannot be assessed. Reference-only plots do not provide patient EMG.

Two operational step-length definitions exist (successive-footfall: each heel's position at its own initial contact; instantaneous: both heels at the same instant) and give different values for some subjects -- the definition actually used is stated next to the symmetry value.
- "Max |z| deviation" is the LARGEST absolute z-score across all 1001 phase points of the cycle, comparing this curve to the healthy-cohort mean/SD at each phase. This is an extreme-value statistic, NOT a single-observation z-score: in this dataset every one of 109 healthy controls exceeds |z| = 2 on at least one muscle, and per-muscle healthy 95th percentiles for this statistic run 3.65 (ERS) to 4.61 (TA) -- higher still under leave-one-out. Do not label a value abnormal by the conventional 2-SD rule; use the healthy reference range given per muscle where provided.
- Healthy-cohort CCI reference (this dataset): RF-vs-BF median 0.68 (2.5th-97.5th pct 0.48-0.83); TA-vs-GAS median 0.39 (0.24-0.59). Composite score reference: median 5.1, 95th percentile 10.7.
- COHORT BACKGROUND (calibration context, not necessarily the exact reference used for this subject -- the actual reference composition is stated in a dedicated line with the subject data below): the full healthy cohort is 138 subjects for kinematics, effective n = 106-109 per muscle for EMG (29 controls have no usable EMG; completeness varies by age), pooling both body sides and all ages; it is not age- or speed-matched to this subject. A leave-one-out run uses one subject fewer than these counts.
- SPEED SUPPORT LIMIT (applies to most stroke subjects in this dataset): the healthy reference cohort's self-selected walking speeds span 0.80-1.79 m/s. If this subject's gait speed is below 0.80 m/s, NO speed-matched healthy reference exists in this dataset -- 41 of the 50 stroke subjects are below that floor. Deviations from the reference then conflate slow-walking effects with stroke-specific control changes, and the two cannot be separated from these data. Describe deviations from the reference as such; do not attribute them wholly to stroke pathology.
- Additional source fields are outside this input. Age, sex and gait events may be available in the release; time post-stroke, stroke clinical scores and a non-paretic-side designation apply only to the stroke cohort, as applicable. Do not claim every listed field exists for every participant. Distinguish not supplied, not applicable and documented absent recordings.

The released paretic-side cycle runs from initial contact of that limb at 0% to its next initial contact at 100%. The cycle boundary may be called initial contact; the endpoints are successive occurrences, not one instant. No intermediate event or phase boundary is supplied.

Model-visible information boundary: the summary contains mean-curve metrics and algorithm-detected intervals, not individual toe-off, matched normal muscle timing, stride-level variability, raw-signal quality checks, or expert plots. Information not supplied here must not be described as absent from the source dataset. Gait-cycle percentages alone do not identify gait phases.

System instructions
Draft a research gait-report interpretation for a physical therapist
or physiatrist using only the supplied quantitative summary. This is a limited-evidence
interpretation task, not diagnosis or treatment selection.

Rules:
- Describe the participant's actual group and side, available findings, and missing
  channels accurately. Healthy-group membership does not prove normal physiology;
  deviations from an empirical reference do not by themselves establish disease.
- Treat detected EMG intervals and coverage as properties of an averaged envelope
  under the stated threshold and duration rule. Do not convert them into physiological
  onset, offset, duration, continuous contraction, or inactivity outside the intervals.
  You may describe reported coverage, retained intervals and boundary continuity at
  the mean-envelope level. Do not infer the same pattern in every individual stride.
  Say no below-threshold segment only when the supplied evidence establishes that;
  a rounded coverage value or no retained interval alone does not establish it.
- Individual toe-off and matched muscle-specific timing references are not supplied
  in this task. Use percentages of the analysis cycle. When the input explicitly
  identifies an initial-contact boundary, you may name that boundary or describe
  numerical proximity to it. Do not assign stance/swing, loading response, pre-swing,
  mid-stance or another event to a percentage, or substitute a conventional 60% split.
  Anatomical muscle actions may be named, but do not map an interval to a gait-phase
  function such as clearance, push-off or weight acceptance. Do not characterize
  timing as normal, early, delayed or prolonged without the required evidence.
- Respect the supplied normalization method. Relative envelope amplitude is not
  strength or force, and average curves do not establish stride-to-stride variability.
- Report CCI as a whole-cycle overlap index of normalized EMG envelopes. You may
  compare it with the supplied empirical interval or a supplied median, including
  proximity to an endpoint. Do not invent an unavailable median. Being within that
  interval does not establish physiologically normal co-activation or normal muscle
  function; being outside it does not establish pathological co-activation, stiffness,
  impaired control or a diagnosis. CCI is not simultaneous activation time, muscular
  force or a phase-specific result. It depends on normalization and envelope methods;
  do not transfer values or thresholds from other studies or algorithms.
- Describe GVS and GPS-like as summary differences from the stated reference.
  Neither localizes waveform direction or phase; ROM alone does not establish a
  clinical restriction without an appropriate comparison. Do not treat pooled
  calibration percentiles as matched leave-one-out normal limits.
- Where using a reference comparison, convey both limited comparability and the
  inability to establish a disease effect or clinical diagnosis from that comparison.
  One clear explanation per report is enough; age and speed need not be recited as
  separate checklist words. A speed inside an overall range does not prove that
  an appropriate matched reference exists. Apply these limits to controls as well.
- The location of maximum |z| is the largest standardized difference from the
  reference, not necessarily the time of peak envelope amplitude or a gait event.
  Above/below reference in an EMG row means relative to the phase-specific reference
  mean at that location, not relative to a healthy percentile limit.
- Preserve numerical direction and the displayed precision. Do not invent values,
  clinical severity grades, statistical significance, or unavailable reference values.
  Descriptive comparisons with supplied values remain allowed. Near-boundary rounded
  values do not justify classifying hidden unrounded values.
- Clinical follow-up questions may ask whether a mechanism or measurement issue
  exists; do not presuppose it has already been observed. Do not recommend treatment.

If EMG is unavailable, section 2 may simply state that envelope timing, amplitude
and CCI cannot be assessed. Reference-only plots do not provide patient EMG.

Use exactly these four sections:
1. Objective Findings
2. EMG Envelope and Threshold Findings
3. Kinematic and Reference Comparisons
4. Questions for Further Clinical Assessment

# Reference context: stroke/Sub21_P

The exact system and user input files define what the model receives. The EMG and kinematic plots are supplementary expert evidence, not model inputs.
The plotted mean plus/minus one SD is descriptive, not a clinical normal band. Negative lower EMG bands can result from mean minus SD; they do not imply negative recorded EMG.
The max-|z| p95 span in the input is pooled calibration context. Complete muscle-specific LOO thresholds are not supplied; an appropriately indeterminate classification is allowed.
Controls are excluded from their own mean/SD reference, but cohort-level calibration has prior reference-data reuse. It is not an independent matched clinical limit.
GPS-like is a four-channel sagittal composite, not the validated nine-variable GPS. The pooled median/p95 background is not a matched LOO normality boundary.
CCI uses continuous normalized envelopes over the whole analysis cycle. An empirical interval comparison does not establish normal muscle function.
Threshold intervals and rounded coverage describe the averaged envelope. Rounded 100% coverage and one interval do not prove absence of short below-threshold gaps.
Use the group-specific cycle and normalization provenance in the actual input. No individual intermediate gait events or matched normal muscle timing are supplied.
Administrative age and published bilateral speed are not added to this input. Expert-visible plots do not justify requiring unseen direction, phase or morphology.

Actual model input: read before selecting required findings
Candidate interpretation version: limited-evidence-v3.1-candidate
Draft the four-section report using only this subject's supplied evidence.

Metric definitions (read before interpreting any number below):
- All joint angles and ROM values are SAGITTAL PLANE only. Coronal/transverse data are not included.
- The released EMG curves are ensemble-averaged envelopes. For each participant and recorded muscle, normalization uses a shared maximum across the available strides before averaging; individual strides are not independently rescaled to their own peaks. This pipeline uses the released mean curves without further amplitude normalization. In the checked stroke release, the denominator is participant-, side-, and muscle-specific; it is not shared between sides. The local array audit verified 614 evaluable combinations, not all 700; 86 lacked valid nonzero paired samples. Amplitudes are relative envelope values, not absolute neural activation, force, or strength. Do not rank muscles as stronger or weaker from these amplitudes, or infer stride-to-stride variability from an average curve.
- Above-threshold intervals are the regions where the averaged envelope exceeds 20% of that curve's own peak, minimum retained interval 3% of the cycle (by sample count). An interval written as (X%-Y%) with X > Y wraps across the 100%->0% cycle boundary (envelope remains above threshold across the cycle-defining event). An onset of 0% is the analysis-window boundary, not necessarily a physiological onset. "No interval detected" means no region met the threshold + duration criteria; it does NOT mean the muscle is inactive or non-functional.
- CCI is the Falconer-Winter co-contraction index computed on the full continuous envelopes (2*common area / summed areas), so it can be non-zero even when the DETECTED burst intervals of the pair do not overlap (sub-threshold activity contributes).
- The composite kinematic deviation score is the RMS of the per-joint Gait Variable Scores over the sagittal channels provided ("GPS-like", not the validated 9-variable GPS).
- Step length symmetry index: 200*(paretic - non-paretic)/(paretic + non-paretic), in %. NEGATIVE means the paretic-side step is SHORTER. Two operational step-length definitions exist (successive-footfall: each heel's position at its own initial contact; instantaneous: both heels at the same instant) and give different values for some subjects -- the definition actually used is stated next to the symmetry value.
- "Max |z| deviation" is the LARGEST absolute z-score across all 1001 phase points of the cycle, comparing this curve to the healthy-cohort mean/SD at each phase. This is an extreme-value statistic, NOT a single-observation z-score: in this dataset every one of 109 healthy controls exceeds |z| = 2 on at least one muscle, and per-muscle healthy 95th percentiles for this statistic run 3.65 (ERS) to 4.61 (TA) -- higher still under leave-one-out. Do not label a value abnormal by the conventional 2-SD rule; use the healthy reference range given per muscle where provided.

- COHORT BACKGROUND (calibration context, not necessarily the exact reference used for this subject -- the actual reference composition is stated in a dedicated line with the subject data below): the full healthy cohort is 138 subjects for kinematics, effective n = 106-109 per muscle for EMG (29 controls have no usable EMG; completeness varies by age), pooling both body sides and all ages; it is not age- or speed-matched to this subject. A leave-one-out run uses one subject fewer than these counts.
- SPEED SUPPORT LIMIT (applies to most stroke subjects in this dataset): the healthy reference cohort's self-selected walking speeds span 0.80-1.79 m/s. If this subject's gait speed is below 0.80 m/s, NO speed-matched healthy reference exists in this dataset -- 41 of the 50 stroke subjects are below that floor. Deviations from the reference then conflate slow-walking effects with stroke-specific control changes, and the two cannot be separated from these data. Describe deviations from the reference as such; do not attribute them wholly to stroke pathology.
- Additional source fields are outside this input. Age, sex and gait events may be available in the release; time post-stroke, stroke clinical scores and a non-paretic-side designation apply only to the stroke cohort, as applicable. Do not claim every listed field exists for every participant. Distinguish not supplied, not applicable and documented absent recordings.

System instructions
Draft a research gait-report interpretation for a physical therapist
or physiatrist using only the supplied quantitative summary. This is a limited-evidence
interpretation task, not diagnosis or treatment selection.

Rules:
- Describe the participant's actual group and side, available findings, and missing
  channels accurately. Healthy-group membership does not prove normal physiology;
  deviations from an empirical reference do not by themselves establish disease.
- Treat detected EMG intervals and coverage as properties of an averaged envelope
  under the stated threshold and duration rule. Do not convert them into physiological
  onset, offset, duration, continuous contraction, or inactivity outside the intervals.
  You may describe reported coverage, retained intervals and boundary continuity at
  the mean-envelope level. Do not infer the same pattern in every individual stride.
  Say no below-threshold segment only when the supplied evidence establishes that;
  a rounded coverage value or no retained interval alone does not establish it.
- Individual toe-off and matched muscle-specific timing references are not supplied
  in this task. Use percentages of the analysis cycle. When the input explicitly
  identifies an initial-contact boundary, you may name that boundary or describe
  numerical proximity to it. Do not assign stance/swing, loading response, pre-swing,
  mid-stance or another event to a percentage, or substitute a conventional 60% split.
  Anatomical muscle actions may be named, but do not map an interval to a gait-phase
  function such as clearance, push-off or weight acceptance. Do not characterize
  timing as normal, early, delayed or prolonged without the required evidence.
- Respect the supplied normalization method. Relative envelope amplitude is not
  strength or force, and average curves do not establish stride-to-stride variability.
- Report CCI as a whole-cycle overlap index of normalized EMG envelopes. You may
  compare it with the supplied empirical interval or a supplied median, including
  proximity to an endpoint. Do not invent an unavailable median. Being within that
  interval does not establish physiologically normal co-activation or normal muscle
  function; being outside it does not establish pathological co-activation, stiffness,
  impaired control or a diagnosis. CCI is not simultaneous activation time, muscular
  force or a phase-specific result. It depends on normalization and envelope methods;
  do not transfer values or thresholds from other studies or algorithms.
- Describe GVS and GPS-like as summary differences from the stated reference.
  Neither localizes waveform direction or phase; ROM alone does not establish a
  clinical restriction without an appropriate comparison. Do not treat pooled
  calibration percentiles as matched leave-one-out normal limits.
- Where using a reference comparison, convey both limited comparability and the
  inability to establish a disease effect or clinical diagnosis from that comparison.
  One clear explanation per report is enough; age and speed need not be recited as
  separate checklist words. A speed inside an overall range does not prove that
  an appropriate matched reference exists. Apply these limits to controls as well.
- The location of maximum |z| is the largest standardized difference from the
  reference, not necessarily the time of peak envelope amplitude or a gait event.
  Above/below reference in an EMG row means relative to the phase-specific reference
  mean at that location, not relative to a healthy percentile limit.
- Preserve numerical direction and the displayed precision. Do not invent values,
  clinical severity grades, statistical significance, or unavailable reference values.
  Descriptive comparisons with supplied values remain allowed. Near-boundary rounded
  values do not justify classifying hidden unrounded values.
- Clinical follow-up questions may ask whether a mechanism or measurement issue
  exists; do not presuppose it has already been observed. Do not recommend treatment.

If EMG is unavailable, section 2 may simply state that envelope timing, amplitude
and CCI cannot be assessed. Reference-only plots do not provide patient EMG.

Use exactly these four sections:
1. Objective Findings
2. EMG Envelope and Threshold Findings
3. Kinematic and Reference Comparisons
4. Questions for Further Clinical Assessment
Supplementary curves and reference context (not model inputs)
# Reference context: stroke/Sub24_P

Mean/SD reference: 138 controls; method pooled; exclusions: [].

Effective reference counts: GAS=109, RF=108, VL=109, BF=106, ST=108, TA=107, ERS=106, AnkleAngles=138, KneeAngles=138, HipAngles=138, PelvisAngles=138.

The exact system and user input files define what the model receives. The EMG and kinematic plots are supplementary expert evidence, not model inputs.
The plotted mean plus/minus one SD is descriptive, not a clinical normal band. Negative lower EMG bands can result from mean minus SD; they do not imply negative recorded EMG.
The max-|z| p95 span in the input is pooled calibration context. Complete muscle-specific LOO thresholds are not supplied; an appropriately indeterminate classification is allowed.
Controls are excluded from their own mean/SD reference, but cohort-level calibration has prior reference-data reuse. It is not an independent matched clinical limit.
GPS-like is a four-channel sagittal composite, not the validated nine-variable GPS. The pooled median/p95 background is not a matched LOO normality boundary.
CCI uses continuous normalized envelopes over the whole analysis cycle. An empirical interval comparison does not establish normal muscle function.
Threshold intervals and rounded coverage describe the averaged envelope. Rounded 100% coverage and one interval do not prove absence of short below-threshold gaps.
Use the group-specific cycle and normalization provenance in the actual input. No individual intermediate gait events or matched normal muscle timing are supplied.
Administrative age and published bilateral speed are not added to this input. Expert-visible plots do not justify requiring unseen direction, phase or morphology.

For a new-case requirement, cite exact input evidence. Select clinically useful descriptive content without inferring a diagnosis or severity from rank alone.

stroke_Sub25_P

Draft case requirements; participant eligibility remains provisional

Actual model input: read before selecting required findings
Candidate interpretation version: limited-evidence-v3.1-candidate
Draft the four-section report using only this subject's supplied evidence.

Metric definitions (read before interpreting any number below):
- All joint angles and ROM values are SAGITTAL PLANE only. Coronal/transverse data are not included.
- The released EMG curves are ensemble-averaged envelopes. For each participant and recorded muscle, normalization uses a shared maximum across the available strides before averaging; individual strides are not independently rescaled to their own peaks. This pipeline uses the released mean curves without further amplitude normalization. In the checked stroke release, the denominator is participant-, side-, and muscle-specific; it is not shared between sides. The local array audit verified 614 evaluable combinations, not all 700; 86 lacked valid nonzero paired samples. Amplitudes are relative envelope values, not absolute neural activation, force, or strength. Do not rank muscles as stronger or weaker from these amplitudes, or infer stride-to-stride variability from an average curve.
- Above-threshold intervals are the regions where the averaged envelope exceeds 20% of that curve's own peak, minimum retained interval 3% of the cycle (by sample count). An interval written as (X%-Y%) with X > Y wraps across the 100%->0% cycle boundary (envelope remains above threshold across the cycle-defining event). An onset of 0% is the analysis-window boundary, not necessarily a physiological onset. "No interval detected" means no region met the threshold + duration criteria; it does NOT mean the muscle is inactive or non-functional.
- CCI is the Falconer-Winter co-contraction index computed on the full continuous envelopes (2*common area / summed areas), so it can be non-zero even when the DETECTED burst intervals of the pair do not overlap (sub-threshold activity contributes).
- The composite kinematic deviation score is the RMS of the per-joint Gait Variable Scores over the sagittal channels provided ("GPS-like", not the validated 9-variable GPS).
- Step length symmetry index: 200*(paretic - non-paretic)/(paretic + non-paretic), in %. NEGATIVE means the paretic-side step is SHORTER. Two operational step-length definitions exist (successive-footfall: each heel's position at its own initial contact; instantaneous: both heels at the same instant) and give different values for some subjects -- the definition actually used is stated next to the symmetry value.
- "Max |z| deviation" is the LARGEST absolute z-score across all 1001 phase points of the cycle, comparing this curve to the healthy-cohort mean/SD at each phase. This is an extreme-value statistic, NOT a single-observation z-score: in this dataset every one of 109 healthy controls exceeds |z| = 2 on at least one muscle, and per-muscle healthy 95th percentiles for this statistic run 3.65 (ERS) to 4.61 (TA) -- higher still under leave-one-out. Do not label a value abnormal by the conventional 2-SD rule; use the healthy reference range given per muscle where provided.
- Healthy-cohort CCI reference (this dataset): RF-vs-BF median 0.68 (2.5th-97.5th pct 0.48-0.83); TA-vs-GAS median 0.39 (0.24-0.59). Composite score reference: median 5.1, 95th percentile 10.7.
- COHORT BACKGROUND (calibration context, not necessarily the exact reference used for this subject -- the actual reference composition is stated in a dedicated line with the subject data below): the full healthy cohort is 138 subjects for kinematics, effective n = 106-109 per muscle for EMG (29 controls have no usable EMG; completeness varies by age), pooling both body sides and all ages; it is not age- or speed-matched to this subject. A leave-one-out run uses one subject fewer than these counts.
- SPEED SUPPORT LIMIT (applies to most stroke subjects in this dataset): the healthy reference cohort's self-selected walking speeds span 0.80-1.79 m/s. If this subject's gait speed is below 0.80 m/s, NO speed-matched healthy reference exists in this dataset -- 41 of the 50 stroke subjects are below that floor. Deviations from the reference then conflate slow-walking effects with stroke-specific control changes, and the two cannot be separated from these data. Describe deviations from the reference as such; do not attribute them wholly to stroke pathology.
- Additional source fields are outside this input. Age, sex and gait events may be available in the release; time post-stroke, stroke clinical scores and a non-paretic-side designation apply only to the stroke cohort, as applicable. Do not claim every listed field exists for every participant. Distinguish not supplied, not applicable and documented absent recordings.

The released paretic-side cycle runs from initial contact of that limb at 0% to its next initial contact at 100%. The cycle boundary may be called initial contact; the endpoints are successive occurrences, not one instant. No intermediate event or phase boundary is supplied.

Model-visible information boundary: the summary contains mean-curve metrics and algorithm-detected intervals, not individual toe-off, matched normal muscle timing, stride-level variability, raw-signal quality checks, or expert plots. Information not supplied here must not be described as absent from the source dataset. Gait-cycle percentages alone do not identify gait phases.

  clinical severity grades, statistical significance, or unavailable reference values.
  Descriptive comparisons with supplied values remain allowed. Near-boundary rounded
  values do not justify classifying hidden unrounded values.
- Clinical follow-up questions may ask whether a mechanism or measurement issue
  exists; do not presuppose it has already been observed. Do not recommend treatment.

If EMG is unavailable, section 2 may simply state that envelope timing, amplitude
and CCI cannot be assessed. Reference-only plots do not provide patient EMG.
